1. **Identify the black nodes**: The black nodes are labeled "X" and "N".
2. **Examine the immediate neighboring nodes**:
   - Node "X" is directly connected to nodes "F" and "K".
   - Node "N" is directly connected to nodes "Y", "B", and "C".

3. **Find paths from "X" to "N"**:
   - From "X" to "F" and from "F" to "E" and from "E" to "B" and finally from "B" to "N": path is [X, F, E, B, N].
   - From "X" to "F" and from "F" to "E" and from "E" to "M" and from "M" to "L" and from "L" to "E" to "B" and from "B" to "N": path is notably longer.
   - From "X" to "K" and from "K" to "G" and from "G" to "H" and from "H" to "B" and from "B" to "N": path is [X, K, G, H, B, N].

4. **Compare the path lengths**:
   - Path [X, F, E, B, N] has a length of 4.
   - Path [X, K, G, H, B, N] has a length of 5.

The shortest path is [X, F, E, B, N] with a length of 4.

Length: 4, Path: [X, F, E, B, N]

   - Node E
   - Node J

2. **Examine the immediate neighboring nodes:**
   - Node E is connected to nodes: L, F, G, X
   - Node J is connected to nodes: M, K, P

3. **Find all paths between the two black nodes:**
   - Path 1: E -> F -> M -> J
   - Path 2: E -> F -> X -> K -> J
   - Path 3: E -> F -> X -> H -> I -> P -> J
   - Path 4: E -> F -> X -> H -> C -> O -> D -> P -> J
   - Path 5: E -> G -> H -> I -> P -> J
   - Path 6: E -> G -> H -> C -> O -> D -> P -> J

4. **Evaluate the paths to find the minimum cut set:**
   - To disconnect E and J, we need to remove edges that are part of all possible paths.
   - Removing edge (F, M) disrupts Path 1.
   - Removing edge (X, K) disrupts Path 2.
   - Removing edge (H, I) disrupts Path 3.
   - Removing edge (H, C) disrupts Path 5 and Path 6.

   However, removing (F, X) and (I, P) will be sufficient to disconnect all paths.

5. **Check the minimum cut set:**
   - Removing edges (F, X) and (I, P) will disconnect all paths between E and J.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges that must be removed is 2.

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(F, X), (I, P)].

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (M and B), we need to follow a systematic approach to identify all paths between them and find the critical edges that, when removed, will disconnect the graph.

1. **Identify the two black nodes:**
   - The black nodes are labeled M and B.

2. **Follow the edges from node M:**
   - M is connected to L.
   - L is connected to E.
   - E is connected to F.
   - F is connected to K.
   - K is connected to H.
   - H is connected to B.
   - Thus, one path from M to B is: M → L → E → F → K → H → B.

3. **Find other paths from M to B:**
   - M is connected to Y.
   - Y is connected to N.
   - N is connected to C.
   - C is connected to I.
   - I is connected to P.
   - P is connected to J.
   - J is connected to D.
   - D is connected to G.
   - G is connected to H.
   - H is connected to B.
   - Thus, another path from M to B is: M → Y → N → C → I → P → J → D → G → H → B.

4. **Determine the critical edges to remove:**
   - We need to identify the minimum number of edges to remove such that no path exists between M and B.
   - By examining the paths, we see that removing the edges connecting M to L and M to Y will disconnect M from the rest of the graph.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges that must be removed is 2.

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(M, L), (M, Y)].
